is even,
{\left\langle{h},{\mathscr{K}_{G_{n}^{*}}\mathscr{K}_{G_{m}}h}\right\rangle}={\left\langle{\mathscr{K}_{G_{n}}h},{\mathscr{K}_{G_{m}}h}\right\rangle}=\left\|\mathscr{K}_{G_{(n+m)/2}}h\right\|^{2}\geq 0
, and if
n+m
is odd,

\displaystyle{\left\langle{h},{\mathscr{K}_{G_{n}^{*}}\mathscr{K}_{G_{m}}h}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle={\left\langle{\mathscr{K}_{G_{n}}h},{\mathscr{K}_{G_{m}}h}\right\rangle}={\left\langle{\mathscr{K}_{G_{(n+m-1)/2}}h},{\int_{0}^{\cdot}(\mathscr{K}_{G_{(n+m-1)/2}}h)(s)\mathrm{d}s}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(\mathscr{K}_{G_{(n+m+1)/2}}h)(1)^{2}\geq 0,

which proves the result. Moving forward,
\mathscr{K}_{G_{n}}
is the
n
-th order Volterra operator
[
18
,
42
]
:

\displaystyle\mathscr{K}_{G_{n}}f(s)=\int_{0}^{s}\frac{(s-t)^{n-1}}{(n-1)!}f(t)\mathrm{d}t,

hence

\displaystyle C_{n}:={|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathscr{K}_{G_{n}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{s}\left|\frac{(s-t)^{n-1}}{(n-1)!}\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}s=\frac{1}{2n(2n-1)[(n-1)!]^{2}}.

Consequently, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(X_{n},X_{m})={|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathscr{K}_{G_{n}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathscr{K}_{G_{m}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-2{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathscr{K}_{G_{n}^{*}}\mathscr{K}_{G_{m}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{1}=\begin{cases}C_{n}+C_{m}-2C_{(n+m)/2}&,\quad n+m\text{ is even},\\
C_{n}+C_{m}-C_{(n+m+1)/2}&,\quad n+m\text{ is odd}.\end{cases}

∎

Indeed,
\mathbf{T}_{X_{n}\to X_{m}}=\mathscr{K}_{G_{m-n}}
is bounded, thus a valid pushforward. In contrast, the differential operator
\mathscr{L}_{m-n}
is only closable, hence one may have to close it to make it as a valid pushforward in light of
Theorem

5.2
.
However, this simple approach has a pitfall: it fails when the boundary conditions of the processes are incompatible. For the BM and BB in
Example

7.1
,
(G_{1}^{*}\square G_{2})
is not symmetric, let alone s.p.d.,
Theorem

7.3
cannot be used:

\displaystyle(G_{1}^{*}\square G_{2})(s,t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}G_{1}(r,s)G_{2}(r,t)\mathrm{d}r=\int_{0}^{1}I_{[s,1]}(r)(I_{[t,1]}(r)-r)\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}[I_{[s\vee t,1]}(r)-rI_{[s,1]}(r)]\mathrm{d}r=1-\max(s,t)-\frac{1-s^{2}}{2}\neq(G_{1}^{*}\square G_{2})(t,s).

[Acknowledgments]
We are grateful to Victor Panaretos for his valuable feedback. His insightful discussions and pointers to key literature were instrumental in enhancing the quality of this work.

A
Additional Results

Lemma A.1
.

Let
\mathcal{H}
be a separable Hilbert space, and
\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{B}_{1}(\mathcal{H})
be a trace-class operator. Then

\tr(\mathbf{R})\leq\tr((\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}^{*})^{1/2})=\tr((\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{R})^{1/2}),

and equality holds if and only
\mathbf{R}\succeq\mathbf{0}
, i.e.
\mathbf{R}=(\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}^{*})^{1/2}
.

Proof.

Consider the singular value decompostion of
\mathbf{R}
:

\mathbf{R}=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{j}f_{j}\otimes h_{j},

where
\lambda_{1}\geq\lambda_{2}\geq\dots>0
are non-zero singular values, and
(f_{j})
are orthonormal eigenvectors of
\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}^{*}
, and
(h_{j})
are orthonormal eigenvectors of
\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{R}
. Then,

\displaystyle\tr(\mathbf{R})=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}{\left\langle{h_{j}},{\mathbf{R}h_{j}}\right\rangle}=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{j}{\left\langle{f_{j}},{h_{j}}\right\rangle}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{j}=\tr((\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}^{*})^{1/2}),

and the equality holds if and only if
\lambda_{j}\geq 0
and
f_{j}\equiv h_{j}
for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
, i.e.
\mathbf{R}\succeq\mathbf{0}

[
24
]
.
∎

Lemma A.2
.

Let
\mathbf{C}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
be a s.p.d. block matrix:

\mathbf{C}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{C}_{11}&\mathbf{C}_{12}\\
\mathbf{C}_{21}&\mathbf{C}_{22}\end{pmatrix},

where
\mathbf{C}_{11}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times n_{1}}
with
n_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
.
If
\rk(\mathbf{C})\leq n_{1}
, there exists a
\mathbf{N}_{12}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times n_{2}}
such that
\mathbf{N}_{12}\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{C}_{11})
(i.e.
\mathbf{C}_{11}\mathbf{N}_{12}=\mathbf{0}
), and
\mathbf{N}_{12}^{*}\mathbf{N}_{12}=\mathbf{C}/\mathbf{I}_{11}
. Additionally, the solution
\mathbf{N}_{12}
is unique if and only if
\rk(\mathbf{C})=\rk(\mathbf{C}_{11})
.

Proof.

Let
d:=\rk(\mathbf{C}/\mathbf{I}_{11})=\rk(\mathbf{C})-\rk(\mathbf{C}_{11})
. We may assume
d\geq 1
, otherwise trivial. Note that

\dim\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{C}_{11})=n_{1}-\rk(\mathbf{C}_{11})\geq\rk(\mathbf{C})-\rk(\mathbf{C}_{11})=d,

thus there are some unit vectors
\mathbf{v}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{v}_{d}\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{C}_{11})\subset\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}}
that are mutually orthogonal. Consider the spectral decomposition of the Schur complement:

\mathbf{C}/\mathbf{I}_{11}=\sum_{k=1}^{d}\lambda_{k}\mathbf{u}_{k}\mathbf{u}_{k}^{\top}\succeq\mathbf{0},

where
\lambda_{1}\geq\dots\geq\lambda_{d}>0
, and
\mathbf{u}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{2}}
are corresponding unit eigenvectors. Then

\mathbf{N}_{12}:=\sum_{k=1}^{d}\sqrt{\lambda_{k}}\mathbf{v}_{k}\mathbf{u}_{k}^{\top}\neq\mathbf{0},

is a desired solution, and
-\mathbf{N}_{12}
is also another solution.
∎

More generally, the solution set in
Lemma

A.2
is given by

\displaystyle\mathbf{N}_{12}=\left\{\mathbf{U}_{12}(\mathbf{C}/\mathbf{I}_{11})^{1/2}\mid\mathbf{U}_{12}\text{ is a partial isometry from $\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{C}/\mathbf{I}_{11})$ to $\mathcal{V}$}\right\},

where
\mathcal{V}
is a subspace of
\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{C}_{11})
of
\dim\mathcal{V}=\rk(\mathbf{C}/\mathbf{I}_{11})
, i.e.
\mathbf{U}_{12}^{*}\mathbf{U}_{12}=\mathbf{\Pi}_{\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{C}/\mathbf{I}_{11})}
and
\mathbf{U}_{12}\mathbf{U}_{12}^{*}=\mathbf{\Pi}_{\mathcal{V}}
.

Lemma A.3
.

Consider a
n\times n
block matrix

\mathbf{S}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{S}_{11}&\mathbf{S}_{12}\\
\mathbf{S}_{21}&\ast\end{pmatrix},

where
\ast
is an unspecified entry. Then,
\mathbf{S}
admits a s.p.d. completion if and only if
\mathbf{S}_{11}\succeq\mathbf{0}
and
\mathbf{S}_{12}=\mathbf{S}_{21}^{*}\in\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{S}_{11})
.

Proof.

The necessity follows directly from the Douglas lemma
[
14
]
. For sufficiency, choosing
\ast=\mathbf{S}_{21}\mathbf{S}_{11}^{\dagger}\mathbf{S}_{12}
ensures that
\mathbf{S}
is s.p.d. due to (
3
).
∎

Lemma A.4
.

Let
\mathcal{H}
be a separable Hilbert space,
\mathbf{A}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{+}(\mathcal{H})
be a s.p.d. compact operator, and
\mathbf{T}
be an unbounded operator. Then, the following holds:

1.

\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2}\mathbf{T}^{*}\subset(\mathbf{T}\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2})^{*}
.

2.

If
\mathbf{T}\in\mathcal{B}_{\infty}(\mathcal{H})
is bounded, then
\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2}\mathbf{T}^{*}=(\mathbf{T}\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2})^{*}
is a closed operator.

Proof.

1.

Fix any
f\in\mathcal{D}[(\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2}\mathbf{T}^{*})]
. Then
\mathbf{T}^{*}f\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2})=\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{A}^{1/2})\oplus\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{A}^{1/2})
, hence there exist unique
k=\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2}\mathbf{T}^{*}f\in\overline{\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{A}^{1/2})}
and
n\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{A}^{1/2})
such that

\mathbf{T}^{*}f=\mathbf{A}^{1/2}k+n.

It remains to show that for any
g\in\mathcal{D}[(\mathbf{T}\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2})]\subset\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2})
, it holds that
{\left\langle{f},{(\mathbf{T}\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2})g}\right\rangle}={\left\langle{k},{g}\right\rangle}
.
Let
h=\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2}g\in\overline{\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{A}^{1/2})}\cap\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{T})
. Then,

{\left\langle{f},{\mathbf{T}\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2}g}\right\rangle}={\left\langle{f},{\mathbf{T}h}\right\rangle}={\left\langle{\mathbf{T}^{*}f},{h}\right\rangle}={\left\langle{\mathbf{A}^{1/2}k+n},{h}\right\rangle}={\left\langle{\mathbf{A}^{1/2}k},{h}\right\rangle}={\left\langle{k},{\mathbf{A}^{1/2}h}\right\rangle}={\left\langle{k},{g}\right\rangle},

since
k\in\overline{\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{A}^{1/2})}
and
g-\mathbf{A}^{1/2}h\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{A}^{1/2})
are orthogonal.

2.

Due to the previous argument, it suffices to show that
\mathcal{D}[(\mathbf{T}\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2})^{*}]\subset\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2}\mathbf{T}^{*})
, which is equivalent to
\mathbf{T}^{*}h\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2})
for any fixed
h\in\mathcal{D}((\mathbf{T}\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2})^{*})
. Let
g=(\mathbf{T}\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2})^{*}h\in\mathcal{H}
. Now, for any
k=\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2}\mathbf{A}^{1/2}k\in\overline{\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{A}^{1/2})}
, it holds that

\displaystyle{\left\langle{k},{\mathbf{A}^{1/2}g}\right\rangle}={\left\langle{\mathbf{A}^{1/2}k},{g}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle={\left\langle{\mathbf{A}^{1/2}k},{(\mathbf{T}\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2})^{*}h}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle={\left\langle{(\mathbf{T}\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2})\mathbf{A}^{1/2}k},{h}\right\rangle}={\left\langle{\mathbf{T}k},{h}\right\rangle}={\left\langle{k},{\mathbf{T}^{*}h}\right\rangle},

since
\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{A}^{1/2})\subset\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2})=\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{T}\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2})
. This yields that

\mathbf{T}^{*}h\in\mathbf{A}^{1/2}g\oplus\overline{\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{A}^{1/2})}^{\perp}\subset\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{A}^{1/2})\oplus\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{A}^{1/2})=\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2}).

Finally, note that any adjoint operator is closed, which completes the proof.

∎

We remark that, even in the case where
\mathbf{T}\in\mathcal{B}_{\infty}(\mathcal{H})
, the operator
\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2}\mathbf{T}^{*}
may not be even densely defined in
\mathcal{H}
, which is equivalent to the fact that
(\mathbf{T}\mathbf{A}^{\dagger/2})
is not closable.

Corollary A.5

(Hierarchical Barycenter)
.

Let
p_{1},\cdots,p_{m}>0
be the weights with
p_{1}+\cdots+p_{m}=1
. For each
i=1,2,\cdots,m
, let

\mathbf{A}_{i1},\cdots,\mathbf{A}_{ir_{i}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
be s.p.d. matrices, and
q_{1},\cdots,q_{r_{i}}>0
be the sub-weights with
q_{1}+\cdots+q_{r_{i}}=1
. Denote the set of the associated optimal weighted Green’s matrices by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{i}=\left\{(\mathbf{G}_{i1},\cdots,\mathbf{G}_{ir_{i}})\in\argmax_{\mathbf{S}_{ij}\in\mathcal{G}(\mathbf{A}_{ij})}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\sum_{j=1}^{r_{i}}q_{ij}\mathbf{S}_{ij}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\right\},\quad i=1,\cdots,m.

Assume
\mathbf{A}_{ij}\mathbf{A}_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}=\mathbf{0}
whenever
i\neq i^{\prime}
. Then, the set of doubly weighted Wasserstein barycenters are given by

\argmin_{\mathbf{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n},\ \mathbf{B}\succeq\mathbf{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{r_{i}}q_{ij}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mathbf{A}_{i},\mathbf{B})=\left\{\hat{\mathbf{G}}\hat{\mathbf{G}}^{*}:\hat{\mathbf{G}}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{r_{i}}q_{ij}\mathbf{G}_{ij},\,(\mathbf{G}_{i1},\cdots,\mathbf{G}_{ir_{i}})\in\mathcal{G}_{i},\,i=1,\cdots,m\right\}.

Proof of
Corollary

A.5
.

From the proof of
Corollary

4.5
, we have

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{r_{i}}q_{ij}\mathbf{G}_{ij}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}^{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\sum_{j=1}^{r_{i}}q_{ij}\mathbf{S}_{ij}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2},

so the result follows from
Theorem

4.3
.
∎
